# The notices sent to the library listener

The compiler side tells the library side, before a language-visible change lands
and after every landing, what moved: the six ladder rows with the PREVIOUS digests
quoted (a delta alone authenticates only the previous size — D-265), the generated
manifest numbers, the harness's own summary lines verbatim, and the notice number.
The listener keeps its own count on its own board, and its count is the authority;
this file exists so ours can be checked without asking it. The number lived only
in two sessions' heads until 1.5.8b: a duplicate happened once (step 3 was sent as
24 where the board said 34, corrected by the listener), and the text of a notice
does not survive the session that sent it.

## The rule

Every landing notice carries, in this order: the number; the commit and the step;
what it is (the language-visible change first, or "no language change"); the six
ladder rows at the landed commit, each with the previous digest and size when it
moved; the manifest numbers (`nitpick.obligations`, `runtime/npkrt.obligations`)
as the runners print them; the harness block verbatim (`programs`, `verify`,
`floor`, `parity`, `ok N test(s) passed`); and what is next, with whether the
floor moves at it (a re-pin). An ADVANCE notice precedes any landing that
reserves a name (D-239), adds an arm every program must name, changes a refusal,
or moves the floor; it is unnumbered and names the number the landing will take.

NOW, two rules in one walk. (a) THE HEADER BAIL: every declaration parser with a body, a member list or an initialiser tests `p_in_panic` after its header and before the part that recovers on its own -- `p_parse_func` (the contracts and the body), `p_parse_record` (the `{`, the member loop and the close), `p_parse_trait` and `p_parse_impl` (the item block), `p_parse_rules`, `p_parse_macro` (the body scan, the `{`, the loop and the close), `p_parse_extern` (the member loop), `p_parse_extern_fn` (the failure clause), `p_parse_global`, `p_parse_errdecl` and `p_parse_unitdecl` (the initialiser) -- and returns its own kind with empty windows: the header's report is the one report, a body read in panic (whose statement recovery would leave panic and report the misaligned rest as new mistakes) is never read, and the walks that run before the driver stops (the depth pass, the file-header check) read a well-formed node. `p_parse_mod` is the one exception and says why: a member-less module node is a FILE import to the loader, so `mod:error = { … }` bailed was "cannot find module ``" -- a second sentence, found by the kinds probe; its block is read as before, and a sound block read in panic reports nothing. (b) THE RECOVERY KNOWS A BRACKET AND A TERMINATOR: `p_recover_decl` stops at `is_decl_sync_point`'s set -- Eof, `;`, `}`, `pub`, the declaration keywords (`mod`, `rules`, `assoc`, `opaque`, `error` and `unit` among them) and the modifiers `async`/`thread`/`inline`/`noinline`; not the statement keywords, which `is_sync_point` keeps for `p_sync` and a statement's recovery; not `comptime`, which opens `comptime(…)` inside a type; not `#`, which opens `#size_of<T>()` inside one -- at depth 0 only, where `{`, `(` and `[` raise the depth and their closers lower it: a `;` at depth 0 is consumed, a `}` at depth 0 is left standing (the enclosing item block's end), a `)` or `]` at depth 0 is the broken header's own and stepped over; and a recovery that begins right after a consumed `;` skips nothing (DEF-260: the broken declaration ended at its own terminator, and what follows is the next member -- `p_prev_kind`). A macro's declaration body recovers per member as an item block does (without it a member whose header failed left the loop in panic and the member's own `}` ended the macro). And `p_parse_module` eats a `}` or a `;` left standing at the top level right after a declaration that reported -- a body whose `{` was the mistake (`func:f = int32() pass 1i32; };` is refused at `pass`, its recovery ends at the `;`, and the `}` was "expected a type") -- never after a sound declaration and never inside an item block. The parser's success path is untouched: a sound program's tree is the same tree, and nothing past the parser moves. | F81 | 2026-10-21 | — | ADVANCE for 139 (`nitpick-compiler_41`, built by `nitpick-compiler_40`; DEF-265, registered 2026-10-17 at landing 130 from the fuzz corpus's `tr0550`, and DEF-271, registered and fixed here): AFTER A FAILED EXPRESSION, A STRUCT LITERAL'S BRACES ARE THE EXPRESSION'S, AND A BLOCK WITHOUT ITS `{` READS NOTHING: THE STATEMENT LEVEL KNOWS A BRACE GROUP. UNTIL NOW: AFTER A FAILED EXPRESSION, A STRUCT LITERAL'S BRACES ENDED THE ENCLOSING BODY, AND THE STATEMENTS AFTER IT WERE REPORTED AS DECLARATIONS (DEF-265, registered 2026-10-17 by `nitpick-compiler_40` at landing 130 from that landing's measurement of the fuzz corpus's `tr0550`; low: a count defect, DEF-245's family, every file refused either way): `Box<int32>:c = Box<int32>{ v: 5i32, n: 3i32 }; exit 0i32;` inside `main` (a generic struct literal is spelled bare, `Box{ … }`, so `Box < int32` reads as a comparison) was PARSE-002 "expected an expression" at `int32` and then PARSE-001 "expected a type" at `exit` -- `main`'s body had ended at the literal's `}` and `exit` stood at the module level. READ LINE BY LINE FIRST (the row's reading -- the statement recovery stopping at the `{` -- was half right): the expression failed at `int32` without consuming it; `p_recover` stopped there at once (a builtin type can start an item); the next statement failed at the same token (its identical report deduplicated); and the parser's no-progress belt, `p_sync`, stepped over `>` and the literal's `{` and stopped at its `}`, which the block loop took as the body's end. A `{` the statement recovery itself MET was the second face: `Foo<bar>{ v: 1i32 }` refused at the `{` (two comparisons) stopped `p_recover` there, and the literal's fields were parsed as a block's statements and reported again. MEASURED FIRST on a WIP compiler of the queue and again on landing 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def265/`): `stmt_brace.npk` 2 reports, `lit_brace.npk` 3, the fuzz corpus's `tr0550` 3, `as0594` 3, `ty1282` 2. AND A BLOCK WITHOUT ITS `{` READ WHAT FOLLOWED AS ITS STATEMENTS AND TOOK THE ENCLOSING BODY'S `}` AS ITS OWN (DEF-271, registered and fixed here, found by this landing's own sweep -- its first run had five fuzz programs gain a false report; DEF-245's family at the statement level): after a statement header that failed -- `for (int32:i = 0i32; i < 3i32; i += 1i32)` refused at `=` (D-023), `if (x = 3i32)`, `while (x = 3i32)` and `pick (x = 3i32)` at `=`, `loop(0i32, 3i32 1i32)` at the missing comma -- the parser stood short of the body's `{`, and `p_parse_block` ran its statement loop anyway: the rest of the header and the body were read as statements and the ENCLOSING block's `}` closed the headless block, so the enclosing `{` was "never closed" (PARSE-003 at it), or a later declaration's `}` closed it and that declaration was read as statements ("expected a type", "expected an expression") -- one mistake, three or four reports. DEF-265's brace group made it show where it had hidden: the belt kept the later declaration's `}` its own group's, and five fuzz programs whose `main` that `}` had closed (`ct0003`, `ct0170`, `ct0314`, `op0064`, `ty0982`) gained a PARSE-003 at `main`'s brace. MEASURED on landing 137's compiler and again on 138's (`wt/40i/.internal/probes/def271/`): each header shape inside `main` followed by a function four reports, the C `for` five; a lone `main` with the C `for` PARSE-003 at `main`'s `{`. AND THE CHAIN'S OWN FIRST READING: the headless rule alone left two phantoms of its neighbours -- a broken ARM's rest read as the enclosing block's statements (`ct0080` 4 -> 6 reports, `mc0374` 5 -> 8) and a failed `for` header's rest read as a declaration (`ty0982` 4 -> 2, one of them "expected `;`, found `in`") -- read site by site before the texts, each closed below. NOW: the statement level knows a brace group, as the declaration level has since landing 130. `p_sync`, the belt every parser loop calls when a rule consumed nothing, counts `{` and `}` and stops at a sync point only at depth 0 -- a `}` it meets after skipping a `{` is the group's, never the enclosing block's end; and `p_recover` skips a `{` that follows a name or a `>` whole (`p_skip_brace_group`), as the failed expression's literal, and goes on to the statement's end. A `{` after `;`, `}`, `{`, `)` or a keyword (`else`, `then`, `defer`) is still a block: a block statement after a failed statement's `;`, and an `if`'s body after a failed condition, are parsed as they were. And a block without its `{` reads nothing (DEF-271): `p_parse_block`, entered short of its `{` after a statement header failed, is an empty block, and what follows is the enclosing block's, read under its recovery -- a `{` there is the body, parsed as a block statement -- so the enclosing `}` stays the enclosing block's. AND TWO RULES THE CHAIN'S READING ADDED (its first run over 5,241 files put six new sites in three fuzz programs, each a phantom of the headless rule's neighbours): A FAILED PARENTHESISED HEADER IS SKIPPED TO ITS `)` (`p_recover_header`, parser.npk: `if`, `while`, `when`, the `for` and a `pick`'s selector -- parentheses and brace groups counted, a `{` met first left alone as the body's, a `}` that would close the enclosing block left standing, panic cleared so the body that follows is the statement's own), because the header's rest -- the C `for`'s clauses after `=`, `int64:i in 0i64...3i64` after a refused qualifier -- begins with what a declaration begins with and the enclosing block's recovery read it as statements; and AN ARM'S RECOVERY IS TO THE NEXT ARM (`p_recover_arm`, parse_pick.npk: the `,` or the pick's `}` at bracket depth 0; and the arms without their `{` read nothing, as a headless body does), because a pattern that is no pattern (`MyMacro!(a, b) where (a > b) { … }`, a removed form) had its `(a, b)` read as the enclosing block's statements once the headless block stopped eating it -- two phantoms where the statement recovery stops at the name. So a failed `if`, `while`, `when`, `for` (the C form too) or `pick` header, a body whose `{` is missing and a broken arm are ONE report each. WHAT MOVES FOR YOU: parse-error COUNTS in the fuzz corpus and nowhere else -- a phantom "expected a type" or a literal's fields leave the programs named below, each file keeping its first report; no file of the six libraries or the applications moves, and nothing that parsed moves.
